\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

\displaystyle\ =\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}g_{1}^{2}g_{2}^{4}\Delta_{A;\rm free}^{\mu\alpha,<}(x,x^{\prime})\eta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times{\rm tr}_{s}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{1}\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{2}\Delta_{A;\rm free}^{\beta\delta,--}(x^{\prime},x_{1})S^{>}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{1},x_{2})\gamma_{\sigma}S^{<}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{2},x_{1})\gamma_{\delta}\Delta^{\sigma\nu,++}_{A;\rm free}(x_{2},x)\,.

(111b)

Other choices of
\{d,e\}
are not relevant for the present discussion.
We are ready to compute the pressure from the transition radiation
\mathcal{P}_{\rm TR}
using the previous expressions but with the subscript “
\supset
TR” replaced by “
{\rm TR}
”. Taking the Fourier transform, we get

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\widetilde{\pi}^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm TR}(q)

\displaystyle\subset\frac{g_{1}^{2}g_{2}^{4}}{4}\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\alpha\beta}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{4}k_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{4}k_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{4}k_{3}}{(2\pi)^{4}}(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{(4)}(\hat{q}+k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})

\displaystyle\times{\rm tr}_{s}\left[\widetilde{\Delta}_{A;\rm free}^{\mu\alpha,>}(k_{3})\widetilde{\Delta}_{A;\rm free}^{\beta\delta,++}(k_{1}-k_{2})\widetilde{S}^{<}_{\psi;\rm free}(k_{1})\gamma_{\sigma}\widetilde{S}^{>}_{\psi;\rm free}(k_{2})\gamma_{\delta}\widetilde{\Delta}_{A;\rm free}^{\sigma\nu,--}({k_{1}-k_{2}})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\widetilde{\Delta}_{A;\rm free}^{\mu\alpha,<}(k_{3})\widetilde{\Delta}_{A;\rm free}^{\beta\delta,--}(k_{1}-k_{2})\widetilde{S}^{>}_{\psi;\rm free}(k_{1})\gamma_{\sigma}\widetilde{S}^{<}_{\psi;\rm free}(k_{2})\gamma_{\delta}\widetilde{\Delta}_{A;\rm free}^{\sigma\nu,++}({k_{1}-k_{2}})\right]\,.

(112)

For our purpose, we will take
q=\hat{q}\equiv(0,0,0,q^{z})
, which is the external four-momentum provided by the bubble wall.
One can already compare this form with Eq. (
54
). We have used “
\subset
” instead of “
=
” above because the RHS contains all processes allowed by the vertex
g_{1}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\psi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\gamma_{\mu}A^{\mu}\psi
and kinematics. For example, one can have
\psi+A\rightarrow\psi
or
A\rightarrow\psi+\bar{\psi}
if kinematics allows. We wish to extract the transition radiation process
\psi\rightarrow\psi+A
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\psi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\rightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\psi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu+A
.
To this end, we rewrite the Fourier transform of the free fermion Wightman functions as

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{\psi}^{<}(k)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\pi(\not{k}+m_{\psi})}{2E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(\psi)}}\left[\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}-E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(\psi)})f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k})}_{\rm incoming\ fermion}-\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}+E_{\mathbf{k}})(1-\bar{f}_{\psi}(-\mathbf{k}))}_{\rm outgoing\ antifermion}\right]\,,

(113a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{\psi}^{>}(k)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\pi(\not{k}+m_{\psi})}{2E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(\psi)}}\left[-\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}-E^{(\psi)}_{\mathbf{k}})(1-f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k}))}_{\rm outgoing\ fermion}+\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}+E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(\psi)})\bar{f}_{\psi}(-\mathbf{k})}_{\rm incoming\ antifermion}\right]\,,

(113b)

where we have indicated the interpretation of each term. For the fermion terms, we need to recognise the factor
\not{k}+m_{\psi}
as the sum over spins for fermion external states

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm spins}u^{s}(k)\bar{u}^{s}(k)=\not{k}+m_{\psi}\,.

(114)

While for the antifermion terms, we need to make a change of variable
\mathbf{k}\rightarrow-\mathbf{k}
, and recognise

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm spins}v^{s}(k)\bar{v}^{s}(k)=-[-\not{k}+m_{\psi}]\,.

(115)

For simplicity, we will focus on the fermion terms giving the process
{\psi\rightarrow\psi+A}
. The process
{\bar{\psi}\to\bar{\psi}+A}
simply contributes to a factor of two for the friction. To identify the external gauge boson, we can write

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{A}^{\mu\nu,<}(k)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi P^{\mu\nu}}{2E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(A)}}\left[\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}-E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(A)})f_{A}(\mathbf{k})}_{\rm gauge\ boson\ absorption}+\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}+E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(A)})(1+f_{A}(-\mathbf{k}))}_{\rm gauge\ boson\ emission}\right]\,,

(116a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{A}^{\mu\nu,>}(k)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi P^{\mu\nu}}{2E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(A)}}\left[\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}-E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(A)})(1+f_{A}(\mathbf{k}))}_{\rm gauge\ boson\ emission}+\underbrace{\delta(k^{0}+E_{\mathbf{k}}^{(A)})f_{A}(-\mathbf{k})}_{\rm gauge\ boson\ absorption}\right]\,.

(116b)

With these at hand, we can immediately recognise the terms relevant for the transition radiation

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\widetilde{\pi}^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm TR}(\hat{q})=\>

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{2}^{4}}{4}\int_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\psi}\int_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\psi}\int_{\mathbf{k}_{3},A}(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{(4)}(\hat{q}+k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})\,|\mathcal{M}_{\psi\rightarrow\psi+A}|^{2}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k}_{1})[1-f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k}_{2})][1+f_{A}(\mathbf{k}_{3})]-[1-f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k}_{1})]f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k}_{2})f_{A}(\mathbf{k}_{3})\Big\}

\displaystyle+(\bar{\psi}\rightarrow\bar{\psi}+A)\,,

(117)

where
|\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(k_{2})+A(k_{3})}|^{2}
is the squared transition amplitude of the diagram in Eq. (
110
), with polarisations summed over and
g_{2}^{4}\varphi^{4}/4
factored out. From the conventional Feynman rules, one has for the emission of an off-shell photon
A^{\mu}
from a fermion

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(k_{2})+A(k_{3})}=g_{1}\bar{u}^{s}(k_{2})\gamma_{\delta}\Delta^{\delta\beta,++}_{A;\rm free}(k_{1}-k_{2})\eta_{\beta\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha*}_{\lambda}(k_{3})u^{s^{\prime}}(k_{1})\,,

(118)

where
s
,
s^{\prime}
are spin polarisation indices, and
\lambda
the gauge boson polarisation index.

To perform our computation, we shall ignore the finite-temperature part in the gauge boson propagators
\Delta_{A;\rm free}^{++}
and
\Delta_{A;\rm free}^{--}
as these parts will make the virtual gauge boson on-shell which is forbidden by kinematics. After squaring the invariant transition amplitude and summing over the polarisations,
we obtain in the ’t Hooft-Feynman gauge (
\xi=1
),

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(k_{2})+A(k_{3})}|^{2}=

\displaystyle-4g_{1}^{2}\sum_{\lambda}\frac{\big[(m_{\psi}^{2}-k_{1}\cdot k_{2})\varepsilon_{\lambda}(k_{3})\cdot\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{*}(k_{3})+(k_{2}\cdot\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{*}(k_{3}))(k_{1}\cdot\varepsilon_{\lambda}(k_{3}))+(k_{1}\cdot\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{*}(k_{3}))(k_{2}\cdot\varepsilon_{\lambda}(k_{3}))\bigg]}{[(k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}-m_{A}^{2}]^{2}}\,.

(119)

For simplicity and to show that the transition radiation does not depend on the emitter (here the fermion) being massive, from here onwards we take
m_{\psi}=0
.
We now choose the following kinematics for the two fermions (
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
) and the gauge boson (
k_{3}
)

\displaystyle k_{1}

\displaystyle=(p^{0},0,0,-p^{0})\,,

(120a)

\displaystyle k_{2}

\displaystyle=((1-x)p^{0},k_{\perp},0,-\sqrt{(1-x)^{2}(p^{0})^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}})\,,

(120b)

\displaystyle k_{3}

\displaystyle=(xp^{0},-k_{\perp},0,-\sqrt{x^{2}(p^{0})^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}-m_{A}^{2}})\,.

(120c)

In the soft limit
x\ll 1
,

k_{1}
becomes almost aligned with
k_{2}
.
For the transverse polarisations, one has
[
88
,
91
,
92
]

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(k_{2})+A_{T}(k_{3})}|^{2}\approx 4g_{1}^{2}C_{2}[R]\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{x^{2}}\frac{1}{[k_{\perp}^{2}/(1-x)+m_{A}^{2}]^{2}}\approx 4g_{1}^{2}C_{2}[R]\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{x^{2}}\frac{1}{[k_{\perp}^{2}+m_{A}^{2}]^{2}}\,,

(121)

where
C_{2}[R]
is the second Casimir of the representation of the gauge group of the incoming particle. Using
\mathrm{d}k_{2}^{z}/k_{2}^{0}=\mathrm{d}k^{0}_{2}/k_{2}^{z}
and integrating over
\mathbf{k}_{2,\perp}
and
k_{2}^{0}
, we get

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\widetilde{\pi}^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm TR}(q^{z})\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{2}^{4}}{4}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{k}_{1}}{(2\pi)^{3}4(p^{0})^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{k}_{3}}{(2\pi)^{3}2k^{0}}(2\pi)\delta(q^{z}+k^{z}_{1}-\bar{k}^{z}_{2}-k^{z}_{3})

\displaystyle\times|\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(\bar{k}_{2})+A_{T}(k_{3})}|^{2}f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\,,

(122)

where we have used
k_{2}^{z}\approx k_{2}^{0}\approx p^{0}
and
\bar{k}_{2}=(k_{1}^{0}-k_{3}^{0},\mathbf{k}_{1,\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{3,\perp},\sqrt{(k_{1}^{0}-k_{3}^{0})^{2}-(\mathbf{k}_{1,\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{3,\perp})^{2}})
. We can directly substitute this equation into
\mathcal{P}_{\rm TR}
. Integrating over
q^{z}
removes the remaining Dirac delta function and we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\rm TR}\approx

\displaystyle\frac{g_{2}^{4}}{8}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{k}_{1}}{(2\pi)^{3}4(p^{0})^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{k}_{3}}{(2\pi)^{3}2k^{0}}\times\Delta p^{z}\times|\widetilde{\varphi^{2}}(\Delta p^{z})|^{2}\times|\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(\bar{k}_{2})+A_{T}(k_{3})}|^{2}f_{\psi}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\,,

(123)

where
\Delta p^{z}=-(k_{1}^{z}-\bar{k}_{2}^{z}-k_{3}^{z})
. Compared to the Bödeker-Moore method, one may identify (see Eq. (16) of
[
88
]
)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}|^{2}_{\rm(BM)}\sim\frac{g_{2}^{4}}{8}|\widetilde{\varphi^{2}}(\Delta p^{z})|^{2}\times|\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(\bar{k}_{2})+A_{T}(k_{3})}|^{2}\,.

(124)

Again
|\widetilde{\varphi^{2}}(\Delta p^{z})|^{2}
is exponentially suppressed for large
\Delta p^{z}\gg 1/L_{w}
. As a rough estimate, here we replace it with its asymptotic expression at
\Delta p^{z}L_{w}\rightarrow 0
.
From the profile (
A181
), we have

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\varphi^{2}}(\Delta p^{z})|^{2}\xrightarrow{\Delta p^{z}L_{w}\rightarrow 0}\frac{v_{b}^{4}}{(\Delta p^{z})^{2}}\,.

(125)

Further, we have

\displaystyle\Delta p^{z}\approx\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}+m_{A}^{2}}{2xp^{0}}\,.

(126)

Substituting Eqs. (
121
), (
125
), (
126
) into the RHS of Eq. (
124
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{g_{2}^{4}}{8}|\widetilde{\varphi^{2}}(\Delta p^{z})|^{2}\times|\mathcal{M}_{\psi(k_{1})\rightarrow\psi(k_{2})+A_{T}(k_{3})}|^{2}\approx\frac{1}{2}\times 4g_{1}^{2}C_{2}[R]\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{x^{2}}\times\frac{4(p^{0})^{2}x^{2}}{(k_{\perp}^{2}+m^{2}_{A})^{2}}\times\frac{m^{4}_{A,h}}{(k_{\perp}^{2}+m_{A}^{2})^{2}}\,,

(127)

where
m_{A,h}=g_{2}v_{b}/\sqrt{2}
is the Higgsed mass of the gauge boson. The above expression is close to Eq. (24) of Ref.
[
88
]
with a few differences: (1) It has an additional factor
1/2
; (2) Since we have used the VIA, the gauge boson does not have a Higgsed mass in our case, and
m_{A}
is a screening mass due to thermal corrections; (3) The
1/k_{\perp}^{4}
in Ref.
[
88
]
is replaced with
1/(k_{\perp}^{2}+m^{2}_{A})^{2}
. Nevertheless, the similarity between these two expressions immediately allows us to conclude, following Bödeker-Moore’s analysis, that the transition radiation pressure is of the type

\mathcal{P}_{\rm TR}\propto\gamma_{w}T^{3}v_{b}

(128)

in the ultrarelativistic regime. The most salient feature of this result is the dramatic increase of the pressure with an increasing boost factor
\gamma_{w}
, thus preventing runaway in most of the parameter space of gauged FOPTs. A rigorous computation of the pressure requires a precise understanding of the IR cut-off and of possible saturation of the gauge boson phase space. This is left for future studies.

In this section, we focused for simplicity on the case of a fermion emitting a gauge boson. However, the same exercise can be performed for a scalar emitter, like the Higgs boson, or for a gauge boson emitter, like the
W
-boson.

5
Localised bubble equation of motion

In Section
3
, we have identified a self-energy term which descends from
\Gamma_{2}
in the 2PI effective action, namely the proper self-energy in Eq. (
17
).
The proper self-energy term contributes to the equation of motion for the bubble, and that has been overlooked in the previous literature on bubble wall dynamics.
In the VIA, one can have additional self-energy-like terms generated from the field expansion, as we have shown in the discussions on mixing and transition radiation in Section
4
.
These self-energy terms are non-local, i.e., they contain an integral over the entire past lightcone. Furthermore, the condensate EoM couples to the EoM for the two-point functions, which describe the evolution of particles and are also non-local, making it extremely difficult to fully solve the dynamics.
Our discussions on the non-local self-energy terms so far have relied on certain approximations that made it possible to write them in a closed form.
For the proper self-energy term in the condensate EoM, we have assumed an ultrarelativistic bubble wall, while for the self-energy terms generated in the VIA, we have assumed the plasma to be homogeneous across the wall.
All these assumptions serve to restore the
z
-translational invariance in the wall frame so that the self-energy terms trivially localise (see the discussion below Eq. (
46
)).
However, these assumptions are not generic. For example, in many models, the bubble wall velocity may not be relativistic or only mildly relativistic, may never reach ultrarelativistic velocities, and typically the plasma across the wall is not homogeneous in the
z
-direction.

Upon applying a localisation procedure, the EoMs for the two-point functions can be reduced to the familiar Boltzmann equations
[
100
,
105
]
(for a pedagogical introduction, see
[
107
]
). It would then also be useful to have a localised condensate EoM that can be more easily adopted in numerical computations of the bubble wall velocity, e.g., in the open source software
WallGo

[
82
]
.

We perform the gradient expansion for the proper self-energy term in the condensate EoM. This is viable if the bubble wall width is larger than the typical wavelength perpendicular to the wall of the particles, when compared in the same frame,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}<p^{z}\qquad\text{(gradient expansion)}\quad\Rightarrow\quad L_{w}\gtrsim\lambda^{\rm(wall)}_{z}=\frac{1}{p^{z}}\sim\frac{1}{\gamma_{w}T}\,.

(129)

In realistic models, the wall thickness is typically
L_{w}\sim\mathcal{O}(10)/T

[
57
]
. Since
\gamma_{w}\geq 1
, the above condition can be satisfied in many models even far away from the ultrarelativistic limit.
Doing the expansion

\displaystyle\varphi(y)=\varphi(x)+(y^{\mu}-x^{\mu})\partial_{\mu}\varphi(x)+\mathcal{O}(\partial^{2})\,,

(130a)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\varphi}^{\rm R}\left(q,\frac{x+y}{2}\right)=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)+\frac{1}{2}(y^{\mu}-x^{\mu})\partial_{\mu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)+\mathcal{O}(\partial^{2})\,,

(130b)

and performing the Wigner transform, we obtain

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}^{4}y\,\Pi^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(x,y)\varphi(y)

\displaystyle=\varphi(x)\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q=0,x)+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\varphi(x)\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}_{(q)}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)\big|_{q^{\mu}=0}+\mathrm{i}(\partial_{\mu}\varphi)\partial^{\mu}_{(q)}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)\big|_{q^{\mu}=0}+\mathcal{O}(\partial^{2})\,,

(131)

where we assign for the derivative with respect to
q
a subscript
(q)
. We observe that the last term has the form of a dissipative damping term.
Using the relations (
52
), valid when generalized to the Wigner space, we obtain (see also
[
126
]
for a similar result)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}^{4}y\,\Pi^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(x,y)\varphi(y)

\displaystyle=\varphi(x)\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(0,x)-\frac{\varphi(x)}{2}\lim_{q^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{\partial_{\mu}{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)}{q_{\mu}}-\underbrace{(\partial_{\mu}\varphi)\lim_{q^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)}{q_{\mu}}}_{\rm standard\ form\ for\ a\ friction\ term}+\mathcal{O}(\partial^{2})\,.

(132)

Since
{\rm Im}\bar{\Pi}_{\varphi}^{\rm R}
takes the form of a collision term,
\lim_{q^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}{\rm Im}\bar{\Pi}^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)
describes particle processes via the VEV-dependent vertices in the limit of vanishing four-momentum exchange between the wall and the particles.
Recalling that the tree-level mass of
\varphi
enters the equation of motion through the linear term
m^{2}\varphi\supset V^{\prime}_{\rm eff}(\phi)
, the first and second terms in Eq. (
5
) can be seen to contribute as a higher-order correction, although being spacetime-dependent in general, to the mass (or more generally the effective potential
V_{\rm eff}(\varphi)
).
We denote this mass correction as

\displaystyle\Delta m^{2}_{\Pi_{\varphi}}(x)\equiv\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(0,x)-\frac{1}{2}\lim_{q^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{\partial_{\mu}{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)}{q_{\mu}}\,.

(133)

Then at the next-to-leading order (
\mathcal{O}(\partial)
) in the gradient expansion, we obtain the EoM of the condensate

\displaystyle\boxed{\Box\varphi+V^{\prime}(\varphi)+\sum_{i=\phi,\chi}\frac{\mathrm{d}m^{2}_{i}(\varphi)}{\mathrm{d}\varphi}\int_{\mathbf{k},i}\,{f_{i}(\mathbf{k},x)}+\Delta m^{2}_{\Pi_{\varphi}}(x)\varphi-(\partial_{\mu}\varphi)\lim_{q^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,x)}{q_{\mu}}=0\,.}

(134)

This equation can be immediately applied for numerical computation of the bubble wall velocity.
To have an expression of the corresponding frictional pressure, we look at the last term in the wall frame. In this case, both
\varphi
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}
do not depend on
t
and
\mathbf{x}_{\perp}
.
Using the same notation as in the previous sections, we write explicitly

\displaystyle(\partial_{\mu}\varphi)\lim_{q^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q,z)}{q_{\mu}}=(\partial_{z}\varphi)\lim_{q^{z}\rightarrow 0}\frac{{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q^{z},\mathbf{q}_{\perp}=0,z)}{q_{z}}\equiv(\partial_{z}\varphi)\lim_{q^{z}\rightarrow 0}\frac{{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q^{z},z)}{q_{z}}\,,

(135)

where we have defined
\bar{\pi}^{\rm R}_{\varphi}
in the last equality.
We then get the new friction term at
\mathcal{O}(\partial)
in the gradient expansion,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\rm vertex;\,GE}=\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\mathrm{d}z\,\left[\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\varphi}{\mathrm{d}z}\right)^{2}\lim_{q^{z}\rightarrow 0}\frac{{\rm Im}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\rm R}_{\varphi}(q^{z},z)}{q_{z}}\right]\,,

(136)

where the “GE” subscript stands for the leading term in the
gradient expansion
.

6
Discussions and conclusion

FOPTs are a promising source of primordial gravitational waves and can catalyse baryogenesis, magnetogenesis, dark matter production and PBH production and have attracted great attention in recent years. A precise understanding of all these phenomena requires, as a first step, the precise determination of the relevant thermodynamic quantities and the subsequent calculation of the bubble nucleation rate from a given underlying particle physics model, see e.g.
[
127
,
128
,
129
]
. Since the phase transition happens near thermal equilibrium, the nucleation rate can be computed using equilibrium techniques, although out-of-equilibrium effects are a matter of ongoing investigation
[
130
,
131
]
.
After the bubble nucleates, it expands and perturbs the plasma near its boundary, namely the bubble wall.
The dynamics of the bubble is a genuinely out-of-equilibrium phenomenon and must be investigated using appropriate tools.
Of particular relevance is the terminal bubble wall velocity, which not only affects the gravitational wave signal produced during the FOPT but also significantly impacts FOPT-related phenomenology.

In this paper, we have conducted a first-principles study of the bubble wall dynamics based on nonequilibrium quantum field theory. We have derived the EoM of the bubble wall (
30
), using the 2PI effective action and the CTP formalism. Compared with the conventional condensate EoM (
1a
), we identify a new phenomenologically relevant term—the last two terms in  (
30
)—that describes the direct interaction between the bubble wall and the particles via condensate-particle vertices, e.g.
\varphi\phi\chi^{2}
where
\varphi
is the VEV describing the bubble wall.

With our complete condensate EoM, we have shown how all the particle processes studied in the kick approach and listed in the Introduction of this work can be captured by the kinetic approach as well, thus filling a standing conceptual gap between the two approaches. Processes of type (1) are captured by the one-loop term in the condensate EoM, as shown in Ref.
[
78
]
.
In the ultrarelativistic wall regime, we have shown that the new term in the condensate EoM is responsible for the heavy dark matter production studied in Refs.
[
30
,
122
,
39
]
and friction on the bubble wall via the type
(4)
processes.
We have argued that processes of type
(2)-(3)
can be captured by the conventional condensate EoM and the Boltzmann equations, provided one takes into account the
\varphi
-dependent masses in solving the Boltzmann equations. By adopting the VEV insertion approximation, we have shown explicitly how the expansion of the propagators in insertions of the background
\varphi
can generate new self-energy terms from the last term in Eq. (
1a
), which can then describe the 1-to-1 mixing and the 1-to-2 transition radiation.

More generically, for slower bubble walls, the self-energy term is non-local, making the condensate EoM challenging to solve. We have performed a localisation procedure to bring it to a local form in Eq. (
134
), that can readily be used in future numerical computations and incorporated into, e.g.,
WallGo

[
82
]
.
Although not the focus of this work, we believe that the
\varphi
-dependent vertices could also be important in the Boltzmann equations and should be considered in collision terms. This will be the focus of future investigations.

Appendix A
Derivation of the pressure induced by pair production

In this Appendix we present the derivation of Eq. (
63
) and of its asymptotic form in the ultrarelativistic limit, given in Eq. (
65
).

A.1
Phase-space integral

The integral in Eq. (
61
) is not a simple decay integral, as the conservation of momentum along the
z
direction has been relaxed, and its evaluation must be done with care.
First of all, we go back to Eq. (
60
), plug it into Eq. (
51
) and use that the integrand is symmetric under
{q^{z}\to-q^{z}}
as well as the sign flip of the incoming and outgoing momenta, to write

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=-\frac{g^{2}}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}q^{z}}{2\pi}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\int_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2}}(2\pi)^{3}\,\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2})\,(2\pi)\,\delta(E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{k}_{2}})f_{\phi}(\mathbf{p})\,,

(A137)

where
\mathbf{q}=(0,0,q^{z})
.
Using the three-momentum conserving
\delta
-function to get rid of the
\mathbf{k}_{2}
integration and relabelling
\mathbf{k}_{1}
as
\mathbf{k}
, we arrive at

\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\>-\frac{g^{2}}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}q^{z}}{2\pi}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\int_{\mathbf{p}}f_{\phi}(\mathbf{p})I(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})\,,

(A138)

where we have defined

I(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})=\>\int_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{1}{2E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{k}}}\,(2\pi)\,\delta\left(E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{k}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{k}}\right)\,.

(A139)

To compute Eq. (
A139
), we introduce spherical coordinates for the momentum
\mathbf{k}

\int_{\mathbf{k}}=\>\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k\,\frac{k^{2}}{2E^{(\chi)}_{k}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi\int_{-1}^{1}\mathrm{d}\cos\theta\,,

(A140)

and choose the angle
\theta
such that

(\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{k}=\>|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|k\cos\theta\,.

(A141)

Then, we can use the energy conserving
\delta
-function to localise the
\cos\theta
integral.
In fact, we can write

\delta\left(E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{k}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{k}}\right)=\>\frac{1}{|f^{\prime}(\cos\theta^{\star})|}\delta(\cos\theta-\cos\theta^{\star})\,,

(A142)

where
\cos\theta^{\star}
solves

E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{k}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{k}}=\>E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{\mathbf{k}}-\sqrt{|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}+k^{2}-2|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|k\cos\theta^{\star}+m_{\chi}^{2}}=\>0\,,

(A143)

and

f(\cos\theta^{\star})=\>\sqrt{|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}+k^{2}-2|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|k\cos\theta^{\star}+m_{\chi}^{2}}\,.

(A144)

We find

\cos\theta^{\star}=\>\frac{\left(E_{k}^{(\chi)}\right)^{2}+|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}-\left(E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{k}\right)^{2}}{2|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|k}\,,

(A145)

and

\frac{1}{|f^{\prime}(\cos\theta^{\star})|}=\>\frac{E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{k}}{|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|k}\,.

(A146)

Plugging this into Eq. (
A139
), we find

\displaystyle I(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})=\>

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8\pi|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k\frac{k}{E_{k}^{(\chi)}}H(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p};k)\,,

(A147)

where the function
H
implements the constraints

-1\leq\cos\theta^{\star}\leq 1\longrightarrow-1\leq\frac{\left(E_{k}^{(\chi)}\right)^{2}+|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}-\left(E^{(\phi)}_{\mathbf{p}}-E^{(\chi)}_{k}\right)^{2}}{2|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|k}\leq 1\,,

(A148)

and

0<E_{k}^{(\chi)}<E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}\,.

(A149)

The last inequality follows from restricting the
k
-integral to the region where the energy-conserving
\delta
-function has support.
We change the integration variable from
k
to
E=E_{k}^{(\chi)}
, using
E\mathrm{d}E=k\mathrm{d}k
, so that we are left with computing the integral

I(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})=\>\frac{1}{8\pi|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|}\int_{m_{\chi}}^{E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}}\mathrm{d}E\,H(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p};E)\,.

(A150)

Note that we already implemented the constraint on the energy as an upper bound for the integration. We should also translate the constraint
H
, onto a constraint on the variable
E
.
We have a chain of inequalities

-2|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|\sqrt{E^{2}-m_{\chi}^{2}}\leq|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}-(E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)})^{2}+2E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}E\leq 2|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|\sqrt{E^{2}-m_{\chi}^{2}}\,.

(A151)

It is convenient to introduce

E_{\Delta}\equiv\>\frac{\left(E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}\right)^{2}-|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}}{2E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}}\,\longrightarrow\,|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}=\>E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}\left(E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}-2E_{\Delta}\right)\,,

(A152)

together with the dimensionless variables
x
,
y
and
z
defined through the following relations

E_{\Delta}\equiv m_{\chi}x\,,\qquad E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}\equiv m_{\chi}y\,,\qquad E\equiv m_{\chi}z\,.

(A153)

We can then re-write the constraints as

-\sqrt{y-2x}\,\sqrt{z^{2}-1}\leq\sqrt{y}\,(z-x)\leq\sqrt{y-2x}\,\sqrt{z^{2}-1}\,,

(A154)

or equivalently

\sqrt{y}\,|z-x|\leq\sqrt{y-2x}\,\sqrt{z^{2}-1}\,.

(A155)

The inequality is always well defined since by definition of variables
x
,
y
and
z
we have
y>2x
and
z>1
, as well as
y>2
which is imposed by the kinematics.
Squaring the inequality, one obtains a condition on
x
and
z
, namely

P(x,y,z)\equiv z^{2}-yz-\left(1-\frac{xy}{2}-\frac{y}{2x}\right)\,,\qquad 2x\,P(x,y,z)\leq 0\,.

(A156)

The quadratic polynomial
P(x,y,z)
in
z
has two roots

z_{\pm}=\>\frac{y}{2}\left(1\pm\sqrt{1-\frac{2x}{y}}\sqrt{1-\frac{2}{xy}}\right)\,,

(A157)

between which the polynomial is negative.
If
0<x<2/y
, the polynomial is always positive, and Eq. (
A156
) then has no solutions. We are left to analyse the two cases
x<0
and
2/y<x<y/2
for which the two roots
z_{\pm}
exist.
For
2/y<x<y/2
, Eq. (
A156
) is solved for
z\in[z_{-},z_{+}]\subset[1,y]
.
On the other hand, if
x<0
, the inequality (
A156
) is satisfied only for
z>z_{+}
and
z<z_{-}
. Yet, if
x<0
we see that
z_{+}\geq y
and
z_{-}<1
, meaning the inequality (
A156
) is never satisfied in the integration domain.
So we compute the integral in Eq. (
A139
) to be

\displaystyle I(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})=\>

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\chi}}{8\pi|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|}\int_{1}^{y}\mathrm{d}z\,H(z)=\>\frac{m_{\chi}}{8\pi|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|}\vartheta(xy-2)(z_{+}-z_{-})

(A158)

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle\vartheta\left(\left(E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}\right)^{2}-|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}-4m_{\chi}^{2}\right)\frac{1}{8\pi}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\chi}^{2}}{\left(E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}\right)^{2}-|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}}}

We find that the integral is only nonzero if
{\left(E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}\right)^{2}\geq|\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}+4m_{\chi}^{2}}
, a constraint that arises purely from the kinematics.

We now analyse what constraint arises on the momentum transfer
q^{z}
from this requirement, in particular recalling
\mathbf{q}=(0,0,q^{z})
we have

(q^{z})^{2}-2q^{z}p^{z}+\Delta^{2}\leq 0\,,\qquad\mathrm{with}\quad\Delta^{2}=\>4m_{\chi}^{2}-m_{\phi}^{2}

(A159)

which is solved by

q^{z}\in[\tilde{q}_{-},\tilde{q}_{+}]\,,\qquad\mathrm{where}\quad\tilde{q}_{\pm}=\>p^{z}\pm\sqrt{(p^{z})^{2}-\Delta^{2}}\,.

(A160)

This only has a solution if
(p^{z})^{2}\geq\Delta^{2}
. For the moment, let us assume
m_{\phi}^{2}<4m_{\chi}^{2}
, i.e.
\Delta^{2}>0
. Note that this is in line with the rest of our calculations: we assumed the
\chi
-particles to be very massive and thus absent in the plasma.
Then, the constraint on
p^{z}
translates into either
{p^{z}<-\Delta<0}
or
{p^{z}>\Delta>0}
.
In the first case, the incoming particle is
entering the bubble
. The momentum transfer
q^{z}\in[\tilde{q}_{-},\tilde{q}_{+}]
is strictly negative, implying the wall loses momentum to the particle, and friction is generated.
In the second case, the incoming particle is
exiting the bubble
. The momentum transfer is then strictly positive, implying the wall is extracting momentum from the particle, thus accelerating.
As a final comment, we observe that if
{\Delta^{2}=4m_{\chi}^{2}-m_{\phi}^{2}<0}
the decay is allowed at zero temperature. This manifests itself in the constraint on
p_{z}
being trivially satisfied, implying the process can happen at zero momentum transfer
q^{z}=0
. Although this is a good sanity check of our result, we should stress that if the
\chi
-particles are light, they are present in the plasma and other processes become equally or more relevant than pair production. We shall not consider this case in what follows.

We can now plug in our result for the integral
I(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})
into Eq. (
A138
) and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\>

\displaystyle-\frac{g^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbf{p}}f_{\phi}(\mathbf{p})\times\Bigg\{\vartheta(p^{z}-\Delta)\int_{q_{-}}^{q_{+}}\mathrm{d}q^{z}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\chi}^{2}}{m_{\phi}^{2}+2q^{z}p^{z}-(q^{z})^{2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\vartheta(-p^{z}-\Delta)\int_{-q_{+}}^{-q_{-}}\mathrm{d}q^{z}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\chi}^{2}}{m_{\phi}^{2}+2q^{z}p^{z}-(q^{z})^{2}}}\Bigg\}\,,

(A161)

where we have defined

\displaystyle q_{\pm}=\>|p^{z}|\pm\sqrt{(p^{z})^{2}-\Delta^{2}}\,.

(A162)

In particular, this implies that the constraint on the component of the momentum through the wall
p^{z}
is not trivial.
By changing variable
q^{z}\to-q^{z}
in the second term, we arrive at the final form for the pressure induced by pair production

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\>

\displaystyle\frac{g^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbf{p}}f_{\phi}(\mathbf{p})\times\left[\vartheta(-p^{z}-\Delta)-\vartheta(p^{z}-\Delta)\right]\int_{q_{-}}^{q_{+}}\mathrm{d}q^{z}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\chi}^{2}}{m_{\phi}^{2}+2q^{z}|p^{z}|-(q^{z})^{2}}}\,.

(A163)

We observe once again that the term with positive
p^{z}
momentum describes particles exiting the bubble that lose momentum to the wall and, in fact, induce a negative pressure.

A.2
Ultrarelativistic limit

Up to now, our calculation is general. Next, we focus on ultra-relativistic walls, to show that we recover the known results in the literature.
It is convenient to introduce the functions

\displaystyle F(p^{z})=\>

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{3}2E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}}\left[f_{\phi}(\mathbf{p}_{\perp},-p^{z})-f_{\phi}(\mathbf{p}_{\perp},p^{z})\right]\,,

(A164a)

\displaystyle G(p^{z})=\>

\displaystyle\int_{q_{-}}^{q_{+}}\mathrm{d}q^{z}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\chi}^{2}}{m_{\phi}^{2}+2q^{z}|p^{z}|-(q^{z})^{2}}}\,,

(A164b)

so that the pressure takes the form

\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\>\frac{g^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,F(p^{z})G(p^{z})\,,

(A165)

where we recall
\Delta=\sqrt{4m_{\chi}^{2}-m_{\phi}^{2}}>0
.
Note that in going from Eq. (
A163
) to Eq. (
A165
) we split the integration domain over positive and negative
p^{z}
, and changed
p^{z}\to-p^{z}
in the latter.
The integral in Eq. (
A165
) cannot, in general, be computed analytically.
We can, however, expand the function
G
around a special point and obtain an asymptotic result for the integral valid at least in the limit
\gamma_{w}\gg 1
.
Let us expand around a generic point
p_{0}^{z}

G(p^{z})=\>G(p_{0}^{z})+G^{\prime}(p_{0}^{z})(p^{z}-p_{0}^{z})+\frac{1}{2}G^{\prime\prime}(p_{0}^{z})(p^{z}-p_{0}^{z})^{2}+\ldots\,,

(A166)

and plugging this into Eq. (
A165
) we find

\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\>\frac{g^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\left\{G(p_{0}^{z})\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,F(p^{z})+G^{\prime}(p_{0}^{z})\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,(p^{z}-p_{0}^{z})F(p^{z})+\ldots\right\}\,.

(A167)

For the linear term in the expansion to vanish, we see immediately that
p_{0}^{z}
should be the average
z
-momentum, namely

p_{0}^{z}=\langle p^{z}\rangle\equiv\>\mathcal{N}_{F}^{-1}\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,p^{z}F(p^{z})\,,

(A168)

having defined the normalisation constant

\mathcal{N}_{F}=\>\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,F(p^{z})\,.

(A169)

Then, choosing to expand the function
G
around the average
z
component of the momentum of the incoming particle, we have

\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\>\frac{g^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{F}\left\{G(\langle p^{z}\rangle)+\frac{1}{2}G^{\prime\prime}(\langle p^{z}\rangle)\left(\langle(p^{z})^{2}\rangle-\langle p^{z}\rangle^{2}\right)+\ldots\right\}\,,

(A170)

where the expectation value of a polynomial
Q(p^{z})
has been defined as

\langle Q(p^{z})\rangle=\>\mathcal{N}_{F}^{-1}\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,Q(p^{z})F(p^{z})\,.

(A171)

We refer to the expansion in Eq. (
A170
) as a
cumulant expansion
. Note that it is not guaranteed that this expansion is convergent or that it can be organised in terms suppressed with
\gamma_{w}
, and in fact, it will generally not be. We will come back to this point when we compare our analytical formula to the numerical results.

So far we have not made use of working in the ultrarelativistic limit. Our next step is to find the normalisation
\mathcal{N}_{F}
and the average
z
-momentum
\langle p^{z}\rangle
when
\gamma_{w}\gg 1
.
We use the equilibrium distribution functions in the wall frame, where the plasma is boosted by a velocity
-v_{w}

f_{\phi}(p_{\perp},p^{z})=\>\left(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\gamma_{w}}{T}\left(E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}+v_{w}p^{z}\right)}-1\right)^{-1}\,,

(A172)

where we recall that the energy is
E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}=\>\sqrt{p_{\perp}^{2}+(p^{z})^{2}+m_{\phi}^{2}}
.
The function
F
as defined in Eq. (
A164a
) can be then found analytically

\displaystyle F(p^{z})=\>

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{\perp}\,p_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}2E_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\phi)}}\left[f_{\phi}(p_{\perp},-p^{z})-f_{\phi}(p_{\perp},p^{z})\right]

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{8\pi^{2}\gamma_{w}}\left[\log\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\gamma_{w}}{T}\left(\sqrt{(p^{z})^{2}+m_{\phi}^{2}}-v_{w}p^{z}\right)}\right)-\log\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\gamma_{w}}{T}\left(\sqrt{(p^{z})^{2}+m_{\phi}^{2}}+v_{w}p^{z}\right)}\right)\right]\,.

(A173)

In the limit
\gamma_{w}\gg 1
, the second term is always exponentially suppressed and never contributes. For completeness, we perform all our calculations keeping it, and will only remove it after integration.
We recall now that we always assume
\phi
particles to be light, in particular
m_{\phi}\ll m_{\chi}\ll\gamma_{w}T
. In the following, we drop
m_{\phi}
from the function
F(p^{z})
, allowing us to perform the next step analytically.
Corrections can be computed perturbatively or included numerically, as we will demonstrate below.
By calling
{c_{\pm}=\gamma_{w}(1\pm v_{w})=\sqrt{(1\pm v_{w})/(1\mp v_{w})}}
, we compute the normalisation factor analytically

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{F}=\>

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,F(p^{z})=\>-\frac{T^{2}}{8\pi^{2}\gamma_{w}}\left[c_{+}\int_{c_{-}T\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}x\,\log(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x})-c_{-}\int_{c_{+}T\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}x\,\log(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x})\right]

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle\frac{T^{2}}{8\pi^{2}\gamma_{w}}\left[c_{+}\mathrm{Li}_{2}(\mathrm{e}^{-c_{-}T\Delta})-c_{-}\mathrm{Li}_{2}(\mathrm{e}^{-c_{+}T\Delta})\right]\,.

(A174)

In the ultrarelativistic limit, namely
\gamma_{w}\gg 1
, we have
c_{-}\simeq 1/(2\gamma_{w})\ll 1
and
c_{+}\simeq 2\gamma_{w}\gg 1
.
Since the dilog function for small argument is linear,
\mathrm{Li}_{2}(x)=x+\mathcal{O}(x^{2})
, the second term is of order
\mathrm{e}^{-2\gamma_{w}T\Delta}
and can be dropped.
Expanding the first term, we find the normalisation constant

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{F}=\>

\displaystyle\frac{T^{2}}{8\pi^{2}\gamma_{w}}\left[\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\gamma_{w}-\frac{\Delta}{T}\log\left(\frac{2\mathbb{e}\gamma_{w}T}{\Delta}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1})\right]=\>\frac{T^{2}}{24}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1}\log\gamma_{w})\,.

(A175)

Next, we turn to the computation of the average
z
momentum

\displaystyle\langle p^{z}\rangle=\>

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{F}^{-1}\int_{\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p^{z}\,p^{z}F(p^{z})

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle\left(\frac{24}{T^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1})\right)\left(-\frac{T^{3}}{8\pi^{2}\gamma_{w}}\right)\left[c_{+}^{2}\int_{c_{-}T\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}x\,x\log(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x})-c_{-}^{2}\int_{c_{+}T\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}x\,x\log(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x})\right]

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle\left(\frac{24}{T^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1})\right)\frac{T^{3}}{8\pi^{2}\gamma_{w}}\left[c_{+}^{2}\left(c_{-}T\Delta\mathrm{Li}_{2}(\mathrm{e}^{-c_{-}T\Delta})+\mathrm{Li}_{3}(\mathrm{e}^{-c_{-}T\Delta})\right)-(+\leftrightarrow-)\right]

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle\frac{12\zeta_{3}}{\pi^{2}}\gamma_{w}T+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1}\log\gamma_{w})\,.

(A176)

In the following, we call the proportionality constant
{\sigma\equiv 12\zeta_{3}/\pi^{2}=1.46\ldots}
.

Having found the normalisation
\mathcal{N}_{F}
and the average
z
momentum
\langle p^{z}\rangle
, we are now ready to compute the leading order term in the cumulant expansion of Eq. (
A170
). In particular, we must evaluate the function
G
as defined in Eq. (
A164b
) at the value
p^{z}=\sigma\gamma_{w}T
and expand for large
\gamma_{w}
.
In the limit
\gamma_{w}\gg 1
, the
q
integration limits become

[q_{-},q_{+}]\to\left[\frac{\Delta^{2}}{2\sigma\gamma_{w}T},2\sigma\gamma_{w}T\right]\,.

(A177)

As for the square root factor,

\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\chi}^{2}}{m_{\phi}^{2}+2q^{z}\sigma\gamma_{w}T-(q^{z})^{2}}}\longrightarrow\>1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{4m_{\chi}^{2}}{2q^{z}\sigma\gamma_{w}T}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-2})\,,

(A178)

where in the last step we must use that the Fourier transform of the bubble
|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}
is heavily suppressed for momenta
q\simeq\gamma_{w}T
, which follows from the wall being thick in the wall frame.
Now using
m_{\phi}\ll m_{\chi}
, we replace
\Delta\to 2m_{\chi}
, and the function
G
computed at
\langle p^{z}\rangle
becomes

\displaystyle G(\langle p^{z}\rangle)=\>

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{2m_{\chi}^{2}}{\sigma\gamma_{w}T}}^{2\sigma\gamma_{w}T}\mathrm{d}q^{z}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\left(1-\frac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{q^{z}\sigma\gamma_{w}T}\right)\,.

(A179)

Plugging this back inside Eq. (
A165
), we find the pressure in the ultrarelativistic limit to be

\mathcal{P}_{\phi\to\chi\chi}^{\gamma_{w}\to\infty}=\>\frac{g^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\frac{T^{2}}{24}\int_{\frac{2m_{\chi}^{2}}{\sigma\gamma_{w}T}}^{2\sigma\gamma_{w}T}\mathrm{d}q^{z}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}\,.

(A180)

Using a step wall approximation, we recover the result presented in Eq. (93) of
[
91
]
, up to a log which was missed, see
[
94
]
.

A more realistic wall profile is the hyperbolic tangent

\displaystyle\varphi(z)=\frac{v_{b}}{2}\left[1-\text{tanh}\left(\frac{z}{L_{w}}\right)\right]\,.

(A181)

Its Fourier transform reads
16
16

16

Note that the above expression differs from that given in Ref.
[
94
]
. The mentioned reference overlooked a difference between the used Fourier transform
\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})=\int\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}q^{z}z}\varphi(z)
and the default definition in Mathematica:
\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}q^{z}z}\varphi(z)
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})=\frac{\mathrm{i}v_{b}L_{w}\pi}{2}\text{csch}\bigg(\frac{\pi L_{w}q^{z}}{2}\bigg)+v_{b}\pi\delta(q^{z})\,,

(A182)

where the term with
\delta(q^{z})
is irrelevant for the present purpose as it leads to no momentum transfer between the particles and the wall, thus not contributing to
{\cal P}_{\rm vertex}
, see Eq. (
51
).
Inserting Eq. (
A182
) into Eq. (
A179
), we obtain

\displaystyle G(\langle p^{z}\rangle)=\>

\displaystyle v_{b}^{2}\int_{\frac{2m_{\chi}^{2}}{\sigma\gamma_{w}T}}^{2\sigma\gamma_{w}T}\mathrm{d}q^{z}\,q^{z}\frac{\pi^{2}L_{w}^{2}}{4}\times\text{csch}^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi L_{w}q^{z}}{2}\bigg)\left(1-\frac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{q^{z}\sigma\gamma_{w}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle v_{b}^{2}\int_{\xi_{0}}^{\xi_{1}}\mathrm{d}\xi\,\xi\,\mathrm{csch}^{2}\xi\left(1-\frac{\xi_{0}}{2\xi}\right)\,,

(A183)

where
\xi_{0}=\>\pi L_{w}m_{\chi}^{2}/(\sigma\gamma_{w}T)
and
{\xi_{1}=\pi L_{w}\sigma\gamma_{w}T=(\pi L_{w}m_{\chi})^{2}/\xi_{0}}
.
We can compute the integral analytically, and expand it for small
\xi_{0}
and large
\xi_{1}
. We find

\displaystyle\int_{\xi_{0}}^{\xi_{1}}\mathrm{d}\xi\,\xi\,\mathrm{csch}^{2}\xi\left(1-\frac{\xi_{0}}{2\xi}\right)=\>

\displaystyle\xi_{0}\coth\xi_{0}-\xi_{1}\coth\xi_{1}+\log\frac{\sinh\xi_{1}}{\sinh\xi_{0}}-\frac{\xi_{0}}{2}\left(\coth\xi_{0}-\coth\xi_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle 1-\log 2\xi_{0}-\frac{1}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\xi_{0},\mathrm{e}^{-2\xi_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle\log\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{e}}\sigma\gamma_{w}T}{2\pi Lm_{\chi}^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\pi Lm_{\chi}^{2}}{\gamma_{w}T},\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi L\sigma\gamma_{w}T}\right)\,.

(A184)

Putting everything together, we have an analytic expression for the contribution to the pressure from pair production in the ultra-relativistic limit, and it reads

\mathcal{P}^{\gamma_{w}\to\infty}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\,\frac{g^{2}v_{b}^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\frac{T^{2}}{24}\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{e}}\sigma\gamma_{w}T}{2\pi L_{w}m_{\chi}^{2}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1}\log\gamma_{w})\,.

(A185)

A.3
Comparing exact numerics with asymptotic analytics

We now compare the analytic asymptotic formula we have obtained in Eq. (
A185
) with the full expression for the pressure given in Eq. (
A163
), which we compute numerically.
For the numerical evaluation, we use the formula for the pressure in Eq. (
A165
), where the analytical expression for
F
is given in Eq. (
A173
). Just as we did in our analytical expansion, we take
{m_{\phi}\to 0}
and consider a tanh wall profile. Note that different values of the mass
m_{\phi}
are taken into account in Fig.
3
in Section
3.3
.

Figure
5
shows the comparison between the numerical evaluation of the full result and the analytical approximation of the pressure for ultrarelativistic walls.
The temperature has been chosen as the reference scale by setting
T=1
. The figure shows the results for some reference values for
L_{w}
and
m_{\chi}
. Changing these values within the validity of our approximations does not affect the qualitative behaviour.
Alas, the asymptotic behaviour is the same, but we are off by a constant term!
And yet we made sure to keep our expansion under control, and we estimate the error to be of
\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1}\log\gamma_{w})
.

Figure 5
:
Pressure induced by pair production obtained via the numerical evaluation of Eq. (
A165
) (continuous) compared to the analytical approximation in Eq. (
A185
). Reference values
T=1
,
L_{w}=1
and
m_{\chi}=5
.

To explain the constant offset that we observe in Fig.
5
, we look back at the expansion in Eq. (
A170
) with greater suspicion and wonder whether this made sense at all.
The asymptotic behaviour of the second derivative of
G
for
\gamma_{w}\gg 1
is readily evaluated

G(\langle p^{z}\rangle)\overset{\langle p^{z}\rangle\gg 1}{\sim}\log\langle p^{z}\rangle\sim\log\gamma_{w}\longrightarrow G^{\prime\prime}(\langle p^{z}\rangle)\sim\frac{1}{\gamma_{w}^{2}}\,,

(A186)

which seems to suggest that the expansion is convergent in the ultrarelativistic limit.
However, let us compute the second cumulant of
F
, which is multiplying
G^{\prime\prime}(\langle p^{z}\rangle)
in the cumulant expansion of Eq. (
A170
)

\displaystyle\langle(p^{z})^{2}\rangle=\>

\displaystyle\left(\frac{24}{T^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w}^{-1})\right)\left(-\frac{T^{4}}{8\pi^{2}\gamma_{w}}\right)\left[c_{+}^{3}\int_{c_{-}\Delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}x\,x^{2}\log(1-e^{-x})-(+\leftrightarrow-)\right]

\displaystyle=\>

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi^{2}}{15}\gamma_{w}^{2}T^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{w})\,,

(A187)

from which we compute the second cumulant to leading order in the Lorentz factor

\langle(p^{z})^{2}\rangle-\langle p^{z}\rangle^{2}=\>\left(\frac{8\pi^{2}}{15}-\left(\frac{12\zeta_{3}}{\pi^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\gamma_{w}^{2}T^{2}=\>3.13\ldots\times\gamma_{w}^{2}T^{2}\,,

(A188)

which multiplied by the second derivative
G^{\prime\prime}(\langle p^{z}\rangle)
yields a factor of order one.
More dramatically, we can easily convince ourselves that each higher-order term will also be of the same order, namely independent of
\gamma_{w}T
in the ultrarelativistic limit.
The series can still be expected to converge, thanks to the factorial suppression of higher-order terms, but it will converge to a constant term that cannot be dropped.
This means that the cumulant expansion of Eq. (
A170
) in the ultrarelativistic limit can be rewritten as

\mathcal{P}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}=\>\frac{g^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{F}\left\{G(\langle p^{z}\rangle)+\mathcal{O}(1)\right\}\,.

(A189)

We understand that the cumulant expansion in Eq. (
A170
) cannot be organised in terms that are less and less relevant as
\gamma_{w}
grows, as only the leading order term depends on the Lorentz factor in this limit. It follows, that the constant
\mathcal{O}(1)
term cannot possibly be fixed in this expansion, as it would require computing all higher-order terms.
Thus, it is no surprise that in Fig.
5
, we see an offset by a constant, and it would have been baffling otherwise.

To make the comparison between numerical and analytical more quantitative, we fix the constant by demanding that the numerical and the analytical estimates overlap in the asymptotic region
\gamma_{w}\ggg 1
. Interestingly, it is sufficient to eliminate the factor
\sqrt{e}\sigma
from the argument of the logarithm in Eq. (
A185
), namely, we use the pressure

\mathcal{P}^{\gamma_{w}\to\infty}_{\phi\rightarrow\chi\chi}\approx\,\frac{g^{2}v_{b}^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\frac{T^{2}}{24}\log\left(\frac{\gamma_{w}T}{2\pi L_{w}m_{\chi}^{2}}\right)\,.

(A190)

The comparison is then plotted in Fig.
6
.
We see that even for large relativistic factors
\gamma_{w}\sim 250
the analytic formula underestimates the contribution of pair production by a factor of two.

Figure 6
:
Left:
Pressure induced by pair production obtained via the numerical evaluation of Eq. (
A165
) (continuous) compared to the analytical approximation in Eq. (
A190
). Reference values
T=1
,
L_{w}=1
and
m_{\chi}=5
.

Right:
Ratio of the pressure obtained numerically and analytically in the region where the latter expression is valid.

Appendix B
Field expansion for the two-point functions

In this appendix, we show that the expansion of the CTP propagators that we have performed in the main text fulfills Dyson-Schwinger equations order by order in the coupling expansion and the VEV insertion approximation.

B.1
Scalar propagator

We start from the tree-level EoM for the two-point functions

\displaystyle-\sum_{j}(\Box_{x}\delta_{ij}+m_{i}^{2}\delta_{ij}+\delta M_{ij}(\varphi))\Delta^{ab}_{\,jk}(x,y;\varphi)=\mathrm{i}\,a\delta_{ik}\delta^{ab}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)\,,

(B191)

where
\delta M_{ij}
includes perturbatively small off-diagonal components that are responsible for the mixing.
We now show that Eq. (
B191
) is satisfied with the expansion presented in e.g., Eq. (
72
), namely

\displaystyle\Delta^{ab}_{jk}(x_{1},x_{2};\varphi)=\Delta^{ab}_{jk}(x_{1},x_{2};0)-\mathrm{i}\sum_{c,\kappa,\ell}c\,\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}\,\Delta^{ac}_{j\kappa}(x_{1},x^{\prime};0)\delta M_{\kappa\ell}\Delta^{cb}_{\ell k}(x^{\prime},x_{2};0)+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{\delta M_{jk}}{m_{i}^{2}}\bigg)^{2}\,.

(B192)

At zeroth order in the mixing, the two-point functions
\Delta^{ab}_{ij}(x,y;0)
fulfill

\displaystyle-\sum_{j}\delta_{ij}(\Box_{x}+m_{i}^{2})\Delta^{ab}_{\,jk}(x,y;0)=\mathrm{i}\,a\delta_{ik}\delta^{ab}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)\,.

(B193)

By applying the operator
\Box_{x}\delta_{ij}+m_{i}^{2}\delta_{ij}+\delta M_{ij}(\varphi)
on Eq. (
B192
), one obtains at leading order in the expanding parameter

\displaystyle-\sum_{j}(\Box_{x}\delta_{ij}+m_{i}^{2}\delta_{ij}+\delta M_{ij}(\varphi))\Delta^{ab}_{jk}(x,y;\varphi)=\mathrm{i}\,a\delta_{ik}\delta^{ab}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)-\sum_{j}\delta M_{ij}(\varphi)\Delta^{ab}_{\,jk}(x,y;0)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathrm{i}\,\sum_{c,j\kappa,\ell}c\,\int\mathrm{d}^{4}z\,\delta_{ij}(\Box_{x}+m_{i}^{2})\Delta^{ac}_{\,j\kappa}(x,z;0)\delta M_{\kappa\ell}(\varphi)\Delta^{cb}_{\ell\,k}(z,y;0)\,.

(B194)

The last two terms cancel, and Eq. (
B191
) is satisfied.

B.2
Gauge boson propagator

Let us first restate the Lagrangian of the model containing gauge bosons and fermions

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}-g_{1}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\mu}A^{\mu}\psi+\frac{g_{2}^{2}\varphi^{2}}{4}A_{\mu}A^{\mu}\,.

(B195)

Free expansion.

In the main text, we performed two controlled expansions of the two-point functions of the gauge bosons in Eq. (
4.2.2
) (around a free theory
g_{1}\to 0
) and Eq. (
4.2.2
) (around a massless gauge boson
g_{2}\to 0
). The EoM governing the exact evolution of the two-point functions is given in the Feynman gauge
\xi=1
by

\displaystyle\Box_{x}\Delta_{A}^{\mu\nu,ab}(x,y)-\sum_{c}c\int\mathrm{d}^{4}z\,\Pi_{A}^{\mu\alpha,ac}(x,z)\Delta_{A}^{\alpha\nu,cb}(z,y)=\mathrm{i}a\delta^{ab}{\eta^{\mu\nu}}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)\,,

(B196)

where we defined the
particle self-energies
17
17

17

Note that the condensate self-energy
\Pi_{\varphi}
, defined in Eq. (
17
), and the particle self-energy
\Pi_{\phi}
are distinguished from each other, as pointed out in Ref.
[
107
]
.

\displaystyle\Pi^{\alpha\beta,cd}_{A}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\equiv-(cd)\frac{\delta\Gamma_{2}[\varphi,\Delta_{A}]}{\delta\Delta_{A}^{\alpha\beta,dc}(x^{\prime},x)}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}g_{1}^{2}{\rm tr_{s}}S^{cd}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{1},x_{2})\gamma_{\beta}S^{dc}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{2},x_{1})\gamma_{\alpha}+\mathcal{O}(g^{4}_{1})\,.

(B197)

We first consider the expansion in the fermion-gauge-boson interaction, setting
\varphi=0
. The free propagator satisfies

\displaystyle\Box_{x}\Delta^{\mu\nu,ab}_{A;\rm free}(x,y;0)=\mathrm{i}a\delta^{ab}\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)\,.

(B198)

We now show that the following expression,

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mu\nu,ab}_{A}(x,y;0)=\Delta_{A,\rm free}^{\mu\nu,ab}(x,y)

\displaystyle+g_{1}^{2}{\rm tr_{s}}\sum_{cd}(cd)\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{1}\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{2}\,\Delta_{A;\rm free}^{\mu\alpha,ac}(x,x_{1})S^{cd}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{1},x_{2})\gamma_{\beta}S^{dc}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{2},x_{1})\gamma_{\alpha}\Delta^{\beta\nu,db}_{A;\rm free}(x_{2},y)+\mathcal{O}(g^{4}_{1})\,,

(B199)

satisfies the EoM (
B196
).
Applying the operator
\Box_{x}
on the above equation, one obtains

\displaystyle\Box_{x}\Delta^{\mu\nu,ab}_{A}(x,y)=\mathrm{i}c^{ab}\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)

\displaystyle+g_{1}^{2}\sum_{cd}(cd)\,{\rm tr_{s}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{1}\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{2}\,\Box_{x}\Delta_{A;\rm free}^{\mu\alpha,ac}(x,x_{1})S^{cd}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{1},x_{2})\gamma_{\beta}S^{dc}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{2},x_{1})\gamma_{\alpha}\Delta^{\beta\nu,db}_{A;\rm free}(x_{2},y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}a\delta^{ab}\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)+\mathrm{i}g_{1}^{2}\sum_{cd}(cd)a\delta^{ac}\delta^{\mu\alpha}{\rm tr_{s}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{2}\,S^{cd}_{\psi;\rm free}(x,x_{2})\gamma_{\beta}S^{dc}_{\psi;\rm free}(x_{2},x)\gamma_{\alpha}\Delta^{\beta\nu,db}_{A;\rm free}(x_{2},y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}c^{ab}\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)+\sum_{d}d\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x_{2}\,{\Pi_{A}^{\mu\beta,ad}}(x,x_{2})\Delta_{A}^{\beta\nu,db}(x_{2},y)+\mathcal{O}(g_{1}^{4})\,.

(B200)

Field expansion.

Next, let us turn to the field expansion. We will now show that the expansion in Eq. (
4.2.2
),

\displaystyle\Delta^{ab}_{A}(x,y;\varphi)=\Delta_{A}^{ab}(x,y;0)+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\sum_{c}c\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}\,\Delta_{A}^{ac}(x,x^{\prime};0)g_{2}^{2}(\varphi^{c}(x^{\prime}))^{2}\Delta^{cb}_{A}(x^{\prime},y;0)+\mathcal{O}((g_{2}\varphi)^{4})\,,

(B201)

where we have neglected the
\mu
,
\nu
indices, is valid at leading order.
The EoMs for the two-point functions are

\displaystyle(\Box_{x}+g_{2}^{2}(\varphi^{a})^{2}/2)\Delta^{ab}_{A}(x,y;\varphi)=\mathrm{i}c^{ab}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)\,,\quad\Box_{x}\Delta^{ab}_{A}(x,y;0)=\mathrm{i}c^{ab}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)\,.

(B202)

Applying the operator
(\Box_{x}+g_{2}^{2}(\varphi^{a})^{2}/2)
on the expansion in Eq. (
B201
), we obtain

\displaystyle(\Box_{x}+g_{2}^{2}(\varphi^{a})^{2}/2)\Delta^{ab}_{A}(x,y;\varphi)=\mathrm{i}c^{ab}\delta^{(4)}(x-y)+\mathcal{O}((g_{2}\varphi)^{4})\,.

(B203)

where we used the fact that

\displaystyle\sum_{c}c\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}\,\Box_{x}\Delta_{A}^{ac}(x,x^{\prime};0)g_{2}^{2}(\varphi^{c}(x^{\prime}))^{2}\Delta^{cb}_{A}(x^{\prime},y;0)=\mathrm{i}g_{2}^{2}(\varphi^{a})^{2}\Delta^{ab}_{A}(x,y;0)\,.

(B204)

This verifies the validity of our expansion.

In Appendix
C
, we will compute the pressure from the up-scattering to a heavier fermion. This will require expanding the fermion CTP propagator. The proof of the validity of this expansion follows the same line as for the scalar and gauge propagators.

Appendix C
Pressure from fermion mixing

In the main text, we have studied the pressure coming from the up-scattering of scalars. In this appendix, we turn to the study of the more natural mixing among fermions. As an example, we consider the following Lagrangian

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{\rm mix}=Y\varphi\bar{\chi}N+Y^{*}\varphi\bar{N}\chi+m_{N}\bar{N}N+m_{\chi}\bar{\chi}\chi\,,

(C205)

where
N,\chi
are two Dirac fermions coupling to the condensate via the mixing term
Y\varphi\bar{\chi}N
. We consider that
\chi
is light so that it is abundant in the plasma, while
N
is massive and Boltzmann suppressed in the plasma. The out-of-equilibrium production of
N
from
\chi
, can induce a mixing pressure,
\mathcal{P}_{\chi\to N}+\mathcal{P}_{\bar{\chi}\rightarrow\bar{N}}
.

To evaluate the pressure, we start with
[
106
]

\displaystyle D[\varphi]\equiv-\mathrm{i}\,{\rm Tr}\left[G^{-1}_{ik}(\varphi)S_{kj}\right]=-\mathrm{i}\,\text{tr}_{s}\bigg[\sum_{ik}\sum_{a,\,b}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\left.\left[G_{ik}^{ab,-1}(\varphi(x))S_{ki}^{ba}(x,y)\right]\right|_{y\,=\,x}\bigg]\,,

(C206)

for
i,j,k=\chi,N
and where the
\text{tr}_{s}
denotes the trace over the spin space.
In the flavor basis, we have

\displaystyle S_{ij}^{ab}=\begin{pmatrix}S^{ab}_{\chi\chi}&S^{ab}_{\chi N}\\
S^{ab}_{N\chi}&S^{ab}_{NN}\end{pmatrix}\,,\qquad\qquad G^{-1,ab}_{\rm flavor}=\mathrm{i}c^{ab}\begin{pmatrix}-\not{\partial}+m_{\chi}&Y\varphi^{a}\\
Y^{*}\varphi^{a}&-\not{\partial}+m_{N}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(C207)

We are interested in the term depending on
\varphi
,

\displaystyle D[\varphi]

\displaystyle\supset{\rm tr_{s}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\varphi^{+}(x)\left(YS^{++}_{N\chi}(x,x)+Y^{*}S^{++}_{\chi N}(x,x)\right)

\displaystyle-{\rm tr_{s}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\varphi^{-}(x)\left(YS^{--}_{N\chi}(x,x)+Y^{*}S^{--}_{\chi N}(x,x)\right)\,.

(C208)

From this, we have a contribution to the condensate EoM

\displaystyle-\left.\frac{\delta D[\varphi]}{\delta\varphi(x)}\right|_{\varphi^{+}=\varphi=\varphi}={\rm tr_{s}}\left.\left(YS^{++}_{N\chi}(x,x)+Y^{*}S^{++}_{\chi N}(x,x)\right)\right|_{\varphi^{+}=\varphi^{-}=\varphi}\,,

(C209)

where again we have indicated the implicit dependence on
\varphi
of the fermionic two-point functions.
We now can expand
S_{N\chi}^{++}
as

\displaystyle S^{++}_{N\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})=-\mathrm{i}\sum_{a}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}\,a\,Y^{*}\varphi^{a}(x^{\prime})S^{+a}_{\chi\chi}(x_{1},x^{\prime})S^{a+}_{NN}(x^{\prime},x_{2})+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{Y\varphi}{m_{N}}\bigg)^{2}\,,

(C210)

and similarly for
S^{++}_{\chi N}
.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle-\left.\frac{\delta D[\varphi]}{\delta\varphi^{+}(x)}\right|_{\varphi^{-}=\varphi^{+}=\varphi}=\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}\,\Pi^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm mix}(x,x^{\prime})\varphi(x^{\prime})\,,

(C211)

where

\displaystyle\Pi^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm mix}(x,x^{\prime})\equiv-2\mathrm{i}\,|Y|^{2}\text{tr}_{s}\bigg[\left[S^{++}_{\chi\chi}(x,x^{\prime})S^{++}_{NN}(x^{\prime},x)-S_{\chi\chi}^{+-}(x,x^{\prime})S_{NN}^{-+}(x^{\prime},x)\right]\bigg]\,.

(C212)

Similarly, we would have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\chi\rightarrow N}+\mathcal{P}_{\bar{\chi}\rightarrow\bar{N}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\mathrm{d}z\,\frac{\mathrm{d}\varphi}{\mathrm{d}z}\pi^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm mix}(z,z^{\prime})\varphi(z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-\int\frac{\mathrm{d}q^{z}}{2\pi}\,q^{z}|\widetilde{\varphi}(q^{z})|^{2}{\rm Im}\pi^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm mix}(q^{z})\,.

(C213)

One can then proceed with estimating
{\rm Im}\pi^{\rm R}_{\varphi;\rm mix}(q^{z})
following the procedure outlined in the main text.
